# ***THE OLD-JAVANESE***
# ***RĀMĀYAṆA KAKAWIN***
<note>The <term>Rāmāyaṇa Kakawin: an epic poem telling the story of Rama, written in Old Javanese and following strict Sanskrit poetic meters</term>. It is considered the "Adi-kakawin" or the first/exemplary poem of Javanese literature.</note>

## **WITH**
## **SPECIAL REFERENCE TO**
## **THE PROBLEM OF INTERPOLATION**
## **IN KAKAWINS**
<note>An <term>interpolation: a passage, line, or word inserted into a manuscript by a later scribe or editor</term> that was not part of the author's original work. Hooykaas focuses on distinguishing the original 9th-century poem from later additions.</note>

->BY<-

## **Prof. Dr C. HOOYKAAS**
<note>Christiaan Hooykaas (1902–1979) was a distinguished Dutch scholar of Javanese and Balinese literature. This work is one of his most important contributions to the study of the Javanese Rāmāyaṇa.</note>

<image-desc>A circular decorative emblem or seal featuring intricate, symmetrical foliate and floral scrollwork in a traditional Southeast Asian (Javanese) style.</image-desc>

->THE HAGUE - MARTINUS NIJHOFF - 1955<-
<note>original Dutch: "'s-Gravenhage." The Hague is the administrative capital of the Netherlands and the longtime home of the publisher Martinus Nijhoff.</note>

<summary>This is the title page for a 1955 academic monograph by Christiaan Hooykaas, focusing on the historical layers and potential later additions within the Old Javanese Rāmāyaṇa. It establishes the work as part of the prestigious Transactions of the Royal Institute (KITLV).</summary>

<keywords>Old Javanese Rāmāyaṇa, Kakawin, Christiaan Hooykaas, interpolation, KITLV, Dutch scholarship, Indonesian literature, philology</keywords>

## THE OLD JAVANESE
## RĀMĀYAṆA KAKAWIN
<note>The <term>Rāmāyaṇa Kakawin is the oldest and most famous epic poem in Old Javanese literature</term>. A <term>kakawin is a long narrative poem written in Old Javanese using meters derived from Sanskrit poetry</term>.</note>

Hooykaas investigates which parts of the poem were original and which were added centuries later as the story was retold in Java and Bali.</note>

<note>Christiaan Hooykaas (1902–1979) was a prominent Dutch scholar who specialized in the literature of Java and Bali. His work helped define the modern academic understanding of how Old Javanese poetry was composed and preserved.</note>

# **THE OLD-JAVANESE RĀMĀYAṆA KAKAWIN**
<note>The <term>Rāmāyaṇa Kakawin</term> is the oldest and most prestigious epic poem in Old Javanese literature. It is an adaptation of the ancient Indian epic, recomposed in Java around the 9th century. A <term>kakawin</term> is a form of long narrative poetry written in Old Javanese that follows the complex metrical rules of Sanskrit prosody.</note>

->**WITH**<-
->**SPECIAL REFERENCE TO**<-
->**THE PROBLEM OF INTERPOLATION**<-
->**IN KAKAWINS**<-
<note>An <term>interpolation</term> is a passage or verse inserted into a manuscript by a later scribe or editor that was not present in the original author's version. Professor Hooykaas’s study focuses on identifying which parts of the poem are original and which were added over centuries of recopying.</note>

->BY<-

->Prof. Dr. C. HOOYKAAS<-
<note>Christiaan Hooykaas (1902–1979) was a prominent Dutch scholar of Indonesian languages. He is particularly famous for his extensive research into Balinese literature, religion, and the preservation of palm-leaf manuscripts (lontar).</note>

<image-desc>A circular decorative emblem or seal featuring intricate, symmetrical foliate and floral patterns in a traditional Southeast Asian style.</image-desc>

->THE HAGUE — MARTINUS NIJHOFF — 1955<-
<note>original: "'s-Gravenhage." This is the formal Dutch name for The Hague. Martinus Nijhoff was a renowned publishing house specializing in academic and legal texts.</note>

<summary>This is the title page for Professor C. Hooykaas's 1955 monograph on the Old Javanese Rāmāyaṇa, published as part of the KITLV's prestigious "Transactions" series.</summary>
<keywords>Old Javanese, Rāmāyaṇa Kakawin, Interpolation, Philology, KITLV, C. Hooykaas, Dutch Scholarship</keywords>

# **THE OLD-JAVANESE**
# **RĀMĀYAṆA KAKAWIN**
<note>The <term>Rāmāyaṇa Kakawin</term> is the oldest and most prestigious epic poem in Old Javanese literature, likely composed in the 9th century. A <term>kakawin</term> is a form of long narrative poetry that follows the strict metrical rules of Sanskrit prosody.</note>

### **WITH**
### **SPECIAL REFERENCE TO**
### **THE PROBLEM OF INTERPOLATION**
### **IN KAKAWINS**
<note>An <term>interpolation</term> is a passage or verse inserted into a manuscript by a later scribe or editor that was not part of the original author's text. Professor Hooykaas’s study focuses on distinguishing original verses from later additions.</note>

->BY<-

## ***THE OLD-JAVANESE***
## ***RĀMĀYAṆA KAKAWIN***
<note>The <term>Rāmāyaṇa Kakawin</term> is the oldest and most famous epic poem in Old Javanese literature, likely composed in Central Java in the 9th century. A <term>kakawin</term> is a narrative poem written in Old Javanese that adheres strictly to the complex poetic meters of Sanskrit literature.</note>

### **WITH**
### **SPECIAL REFERENCE TO**
### **THE PROBLEM OF INTERPOLATION**
### **IN KAKAWINS**
<note><term>Interpolation</term> refers to the practice of later scribes or editors inserting their own verses or passages into a much older original text. Hooykaas’s research focuses on identifying these "foreign" additions to uncover the poem's original structure.</note>

### **BY**

### **Prof. Dr C. HOOYKAAS**
<note>Christiaan Hooykaas (1902–1979) was a distinguished Dutch scholar and professor specializing in the languages and literatures of Indonesia, particularly the Balinese and Javanese traditions.</note>

<image-desc>A circular emblem featuring intricate, symmetrical foliate and floral patterns in a traditional Javanese style, used as the seal for the Royal Institute for Linguistics, Geography and Ethnology.</image-desc>

->THE HAGUE - MARTINUS NIJHOFF - 1955<-
<note>original: "'s-Gravenhage." Martinus Nijhoff was a prominent Dutch publishing house known for academic and historical works.</note>

<summary>This is the title page of Volume XVI of the KITLV series, featuring a monograph by Christiaan Hooykaas on the Old Javanese version of the Ramayana epic. The study specifically investigates how to identify later additions (interpolations) in traditional Javanese poetry.</summary>

<keywords>Old Javanese, Rāmāyaṇa Kakawin, Philology, Interpolation, KITLV, C. Hooykaas, Indonesian Literature, Poetics</keywords>

<note>The <term>Rāmāyaṇa Kakawin</term> is the oldest and most significant epic poem in Old Javanese literature, dating to the 9th century. A <term>kakawin</term> is a form of long narrative poetry written in the Old Javanese language that adopts the complex metrical rules of Sanskrit poetry.</note>

->**WITH**<-
->**SPECIAL REFERENCE TO**<-
->**THE PROBLEM OF INTERPOLATION**<-
->**IN KAKAWINS**<-
<note><term>Interpolation</term> refers to the scholarly process of identifying "extra" passages that may have been added to a text by later scribes or poets, rather than being part of the original 9th-century composition.</note>

->**BY**<-

->**Prof. Dr. C. HOOYKAAS**<-

->**IN KAKAWINS**<-

# THE OLD-JAVANESE RĀMĀYAṆA KAKAWIN
### WITH SPECIAL REFERENCE TO THE PROBLEM OF INTERPOLATION IN KAKAWINS
<note>The <term>Rāmāyaṇa</term> is an ancient Indian epic poem. In Java, it was adapted into a <term>kakawin</term>, a long narrative poem written in Old Javanese using Indian poetic meters. This study examines <term>interpolation</term>—the practice of later scribes or poets inserting new material into an older, original text.</note>

->BY<-

->Prof. Dr. C. HOOYKAAS<-
<note>Christiaan Hooykaas (1902–1979) was a prominent Dutch scholar of Balinese and Javanese literature and religion.</note>

# ***THE OLD-JAVANESE RĀMĀYAṆA KAKAWIN***
<note>The <term>Rāmāyaṇa</term> is an ancient Indian epic. In Java, it was transformed into a <term>kakawin</term>—a sophisticated form of courtly narrative poetry written in Old Javanese that follows the strict rhythmic rules of Indian Sanskrit verse.</note>

## **WITH SPECIAL REFERENCE TO THE PROBLEM OF INTERPOLATION IN KAKAWINS**
<note><term>Interpolation</term> refers to the process where later writers or copyists insert new passages into an original text. Hooykaas is investigating which parts of the Javanese Ramayana were original and which were added centuries later.</note>

->BY<-

->Prof. Dr. C. **HOOYKAAS**<-
<note>Christiaan Hooykaas (1902–1979) was a distinguished Dutch academic who specialized in the literature and religion of Bali and Java.</note>

<image-desc>A circular emblem or seal featuring symmetrical, ornate foliate scrollwork and floral motifs, typical of the decorative arts found in traditional Indonesian woodcarving.</image-desc>

->THE HAGUE - MARTINUS NIJHOFF - 1955<-
<note>original: "'s-Gravenhage." This is the formal Dutch name for The Hague, the seat of the Dutch government and the location of the publisher, Martinus Nijhoff.</note>

<summary>This is the title page of a 1955 academic study by Professor C. Hooykaas regarding the Old Javanese version of the Ramayana epic. The work specifically investigates how the poem was modified and expanded by later authors through the process of interpolation.</summary>

# ***THE OLD-JAVANESE RĀMĀYAṆA KAKAWIN***
<note>The <term>Rāmāyaṇa</term> is an ancient Indian epic. In Java, it was transformed into a <term>kakawin</term>—a sophisticated form of courtly narrative poetry written in Old Javanese that follows the strict rhythmic rules of Indian Sanskrit verse.</note>

## **WITH SPECIAL REFERENCE TO THE PROBLEM OF INTERPOLATION IN KAKAWINS**
<note><term>Interpolation</term> refers to the process where later writers or copyists insert new passages into an original text. Hooykaas is investigating which parts of the Javanese Ramayana were original and which were added centuries later.</note>

->BY<-

->Prof. Dr C. HOOYKAAS<-
<note>Christiaan Hooykaas (1902–1979) was a distinguished Dutch academic who specialized in the literature, linguistics, and religion of Bali and Java.</note>

## ***THE OLD-JAVANESE RĀMĀYAṆA KAKAWIN***

Hooykaas examines whether certain sections of the Javanese Ramayana are later additions rather than the work of the original poet.</note>

->Prof. Dr **C. HOOYKAAS**<-

# ***THE OLD-JAVANESE***
# ***RĀMĀYAṆA KAKAWIN***
<note>The <term>Rāmāyaṇa</term> is one of the two great ancient Indian epics. In Java, it was adapted into the <term>kakawin</term>—a form of long narrative poem written in Old Javanese that follows the complex poetic meters of Sanskrit literature.</note>

->WITH<-
->SPECIAL REFERENCE TO<-
->THE PROBLEM OF INTERPOLATION<-
->IN KAKAWINS<-
<note><term>Interpolation</term> refers to the practice of adding new material into an existing text by later writers or copyists. Hooykaas focuses on identifying which parts of the poem were original and which were added centuries later.</note>

->BY<-

->Prof. Dr C. HOOYKAAS<-
<note>Christiaan Hooykaas (1902–1979) was a prominent Dutch scholar who dedicated his life to the study of Balinese and Javanese literature and religion.</note>

<image-desc>A circular decorative emblem featuring symmetrical, intricate foliate and floral scrollwork designs—characteristic of Javanese woodcarving or stone relief motifs—surrounding a central stylized figure or object.</image-desc>

->THE HAGUE - MARTINUS NIJHOFF - 1955<-
<note>original: "'s-Gravenhage." This is the formal Dutch name for the city of The Hague. Martinus Nijhoff was a renowned Dutch publishing house specializing in academic and legal texts.</note>

<summary>This is the title page of a 1955 scholarly work by C. Hooykaas concerning the Old Javanese Ramayana. The book specifically investigates "interpolations"—later additions to the original text—within the tradition of Javanese court poetry.</summary>

## ***THE OLD-JAVANESE RĀMĀYAṆA KAKAWIN***
<note>The <term>Rāmāyaṇa</term> is one of the two great ancient Indian epics. In Java, it was adapted into the <term>kakawin</term>—a form of long narrative poem written in Old Javanese that follows the complex poetic meters of Sanskrit literature.</note>

### **WITH SPECIAL REFERENCE TO THE PROBLEM OF INTERPOLATION IN KAKAWINS**
<note><term>Interpolation</term> refers to the practice of adding new material into an existing text by later writers or copyists. Hooykaas focuses on identifying which parts of the poem were original and which were added centuries later.</note>

->BY<-

->Prof. Dr C. HOOYKAAS<-
<note>Christiaan Hooykaas (1902–1979) was a prominent Dutch scholar who dedicated his life to the study of Balinese and Javanese literature and religion.</note>

<image-desc>A circular vignette featuring highly detailed, symmetrical scrolling foliate and floral ornamentation in a style characteristic of traditional Indonesian decorative arts.</image-desc>

->THE HAGUE - MARTINUS NIJHOFF - 1955<-
<note>original: "'s-Gravenhage." This is the formal Dutch name for the city of The Hague. Martinus Nijhoff was a renowned Dutch publishing house specializing in law, humanities, and social sciences.</note>

<summary>This is the title page for a scholarly monograph by C. Hooykaas examining the authenticity and structure of the Old Javanese version of the Ramayana epic. Published in 1955, it addresses the technical literary problem of identifying later additions within classical Javanese poetry.</summary>

<keywords>Old Javanese literature, Rāmāyaṇa Kakawin, C. Hooykaas, philology, textual interpolation, KITLV, Indonesian studies</keywords>
